767Jul 8, 2026Order denying (741) Motion for Leave to Appeal in case 3:23-cv-03417-VC: The motion for leave to file an interlocutory appeal is denied. To be sure, the issue presented in the motion is a difficult one, and Judge Alsup's opinion makes a good point. But it won't be long before a final judgment is entered in at least one of the Meta cases before this Court. That judgment will allow the issue presented in the current motion, along with many other important issues relating to the use of copyrighted materials in the training of AI models, to be presented to the Court of Appeals as part of a tidy package. Signed by Judge Vince Chhabria on 7/8/2026. (This is a text-only entry generated by the court. There is no document associated with this entry.) 500Mar 26, 2025ORDER. The Court declines at this late stage to address the plaintiffs' assertion (in their 487 opposition to Meta's request for leave to file a rebuttal report) that Meta is failing to comply with its discovery obligations. If Meta fails to produce material it is required to produce, the plaintiffs are free to seek relief under Rule 56(d) at the appropriate time (or to argue in their papers that they would be entitled to summary judgment if only Meta had produced documents that it needed to produce). Given the magnitude of this case, Meta is urged to take a "when it doubt, produce it" approach. Signed by Judge Vince Chhabria on 3/26/2025. (This is a text-only entry generated by the court. There is no document associated with this entry.) (vclc1, COURT STAFF) (Filed on 3/26/2025) (Entered: 03/26/2025)

Given the public interest in this case, the parties are ordered to confer no later than two business days after the filing of each summary judgment brief to decide what, if anything, should be left redacted in that brief. If the parties wish to handle unsealing of exhibits on the same timeline, they may do so, but if that's too burdensome, they can wait until the Friday before the hearing to file unredacted (or less redacted) versions of the exhibits. 156Sep 18, 2024ORDER. Based on previous filings, the Court has been under the impression that there's no real dispute about whether Meta fed copyrighted works to its AI programs without authorization, and that the only real legal question to be presented at summary judgment is whether doing so constituted "fair use" within the meaning of copyright law. If that's correct, it's difficult to understand why adjudication of that issue at summary judgment should be delayed beyond March 2025. The parties should be prepared to discuss this issue, along with the broader questions presented by the motion to extend the case schedule, at Friday's case management conference. Signed by Judge Vince Chhabria on 9/18/2024. (This is a text-only entry generated by the court. There is no document associated with this entry.) (vclc1, COURT STAFF) (Filed on 9/18/2024) (Entered: 09/18/2024)
